 ## SETUP — Operator PATH discoverability for `eee` launcher
 
-The `bin/eee.cmd` shim ships with this runtime but is NOT auto-discoverable from a fresh PowerShell session until the operator adds `Z:\claude-sota-installed\bin` to PATH.
-
-**Canonical install (run ONCE)**:
-```powershell
-pwsh -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File Z:\claude-sota-installed\bin\install-path.ps1
-```
-
-This script (`bin/install-path.ps1`, Wave 50 Fire 40 ship) self-diagnoses 4 probes (shim presence / User-scope Path state / Process-scope Path state / install action), applies User-scope `SetEnvironmentVariable` if absent (no-op if present), and reports the exact next step depending on state. Cite-anchored to Microsoft Learn TIER-1 PowerShell 7.6 docs `https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_environment_variables?view=powershell-7.6` §"Create persistent environment variables in Windows" §"Set environment variables with SetEnvironmentVariable()" [VERIFIED 2026-05-06 via WebFetch].
-
-**After running install-path.ps1, pick ONE path to launch**:
-
-```powershell
-# Option A — current session, immediate (Process-scope override):
-$env:Path = "Z:\claude-sota-installed\bin;$env:Path"
-eee
-```
-
-```powershell
-# Option B — clean fresh shell (recommended; picks up persistent User Path):
-# 1. Close ALL PowerShell + Windows Terminal windows
-# 2. Re-open from Start Menu / Explorer (NOT from a parent shell)
-# 3. eee
-```
-
-**Why the shell-restart caveat exists**: Windows reads User-scope Path **at process start**, not on registry change. Existing PowerShell sessions inherit a snapshot from their parent process and don't refresh. This is a Windows-platform quirk, not a bug in the install. The `install-path.ps1` script's [3/4] probe surfaces this state explicitly so operators don't get confused when `eee` resolves in some shells but not others.
-
-**Scope discipline** (per Microsoft Learn §"On Windows, environment variables can be defined in three scopes"):
-- **User** scope (used by `install-path.ps1`): persists across PowerShell sessions for the operator only; no admin rights needed
-- **Machine** scope: persists across sessions for ALL users; requires admin rights; reserved for system-wide installs (Tier 0 binaries like `claude.exe`, `codex.exe`); do NOT use for `eee` launcher
-- **Process** scope: current PowerShell session only; Option A above
-
-**Anti-pattern**: do NOT modify `Z:\claude-sota\bin` (sibling) PATH entry to point at installed runtime — that breaks sibling launcher (`sss`) discoverability. `install-path.ps1` appends `Z:\claude-sota-installed\bin` as a SEPARATE PATH entry. Both can coexist; `sss` and `eee` are distinct shim names.
-
-**Pre-condition for `eee` to actually launch successfully** (after PATH is correct): hard-gate per `tools/eee.ps1:T0.1-T0.5` — claude.exe native install + 3 plugins enabled + 3 plugin caches present + python venv at `Z:/venvs/claude` + codex CLI on PATH. If any FAIL, `eee` exits 1 with error list. Use `EEE_BOOTSTRAP=1` env to bypass during initial install per cardinal-rule-7 graduated-unleash phase 1.
+See `docs/operator-path-setup.md` — covers `bin/install-path.ps1` invocation, Option A (Process-scope) vs Option B (fresh-shell) launch paths, scope discipline (User / Machine / Process per Microsoft Learn TIER-1 PowerShell 7.6 anchor), the `Z:\claude-sota\bin` sibling-PATH anti-pattern, and the `tools/eee.ps1:T0.1-T0.5` pre-condition gate. Extracted 2026-05-07 per /doctor 40k-char ceiling; cite anchors preserved verbatim in the new file (cardinal-rule-1 + cardinal-rule-8 conformance).

.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:31:When TWO OR MORE `claude` CLI sessions operate concurrently against the same checkout, EACH session MUST start with `claude --worktree` (or `-w`) so each session gets its own isolated working tree under `.claude/worktrees/`. The launcher `tools/sss.ps1` forwards `--worktree`/`-w` per `tools/sss.ps1:--worktree forwarding block` symbol-anchor (per `port-note-discipline.md §2 Discipline 1`) (shipped via codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.88 gap-resolution arc 2026-05-02 commit `f3c1e57`) — mandate is now hard MUST, no longer SHOULD-WHEN-AVAILABLE. Without this isolation, one session's `git rm` + `git add` + `git commit` sequence can interleave with another session's pending stage operations, producing audit-trail mismatches where commit content does not match commit message.

.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:115:   - **(c) Lockfile + O_CREAT|O_APPEND atomic-write for SHARED JSONL append**: open the JSONL log with `os.open(path, os.O_CREAT | os.O_APPEND | os.O_WRONLY, mode)` where `mode` defaults to `0o600` (owner-only — per Round-5b T3 round-3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.78 NEW-CLASS trust-boundary finding on commit `c2b5dee`: `0o644` was world-readable and leaked audit-trail content (agent prompts, PIDs, verdict cite paths, `agent_id` UUIDs, T1/T2/T3 verdict bodies) on multi-user hosts. Callers MAY opt-in to `0o640` group-readable for shared-CI cases via explicit `mode=` argument + documented umask interaction (e.g., `umask 0o027` for CI runner) — `0o644` is REJECTED as default for any audit-trail JSONL). **Migration semantic for legacy files** *(Round-7 amendment per T3 round-4 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.91 NEW-CLASS finding on commit `df37fb1`; closes non-migrating gap caught by codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE — POSIX `os.open` applies `mode` ONLY when `O_CREAT` actually CREATES the file)*: pre-existing files at `0o644` (created under Round-3-through-5 mandates pre-Round-6) RETAIN their world-readable perms after open, leaving the audit-trail leak intact unless coerced. After every `os.open`, the caller MUST: (i) read `current_mode = stat.S_IMODE(os.fstat(fd).st_mode)`; (ii) if `current_mode != mode` AND the file is owned by caller-uid, call `os.fchmod(fd, mode)` to coerce existing file to the mode invariant; (iii) if `os.fchmod` raises (`PermissionError` due to read-only mount, alien owner, ACL conflict on Windows NTFS, etc.), FAIL CLOSED — propagate the exception rather than proceeding with non-conforming perms (the audit-trail invariant is enforced everywhere or fails loudly). Document in commit body for any consumer applying this rule: one-time migration shell command `find .claude/state -name '*.jsonl' -perm /044 -exec chmod 0600 {} +` (POSIX) OR `Get-ChildItem -Path .claude/state -Filter *.jsonl -Recurse | Where-Object { ($_ | Get-Acl).AccessToString -match 'Everyone Allow Read' } | ForEach-Object { icacls $_.FullName /inheritance:r /grant ${env:USERNAME}:F }` (Windows) for legacy `mcp_health.jsonl` / `subagent_transcripts.jsonl` / `codex_postcommit_reviews.jsonl` / `subagent_start.jsonl` / `worktree_lifecycle_audit.jsonl` files created at `0o644` per pre-Round-6 mandates. The `O_CREAT` flag ensures a missing log auto-creates rather than failing the first writer; **if `os.open` raises (read-only mount, permission denied, disk full), the exception MUST propagate — do not catch-and-ignore** (per Round-5 F-1 silent-failure finding). Take an **exclusive advisory lock** (`fcntl.flock(fd, LOCK_EX)` on POSIX / `msvcrt.locking` on Windows); **if neither primitive is available, or if the platform does not enforce advisory locks (e.g., NFS without NLM support), FAIL CLOSED — raise rather than proceeding without a lock** (per Round-5 F-2 NFS-silent-succeed finding). **Windows separate-lockfile mechanism** *(Round-7c per T3 round-7b NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.78 NEW-CLASS finding on commit `48c49fb` caught by codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE)*: `msvcrt.locking` locks `nbytes` from CURRENT file-pointer position, NOT the whole file like POSIX `fcntl.flock`. Under O_APPEND the JSONL fd's pointer moves on each write, breaking lock-release symmetry (LK_UNLCK at the new pointer would find no lock at the byte LK_LOCK was issued for). Round-7b workaround (shipped at `scripts/_atomic_jsonl_append.py:_acquire_lock(fd, path)` commit `48c49fb`): open separate `<jsonl-path>.lock` file with 1-byte placeholder + lock byte 0 of the LOCKFILE (no writes to lockfile means file pointer stays at byte 0). Module returns `lock_fd` for Windows callers to release; `None` for POSIX. **Lock-protocol rollout constraint** *(Round-7c per same T3 finding)*: when the lock protocol bumps (direct-jsonl-fd → separate-lockfile-v1, or separate-lockfile-v1 → separate-lockfile-v2-versioned), enforce single-version deployment for the entire concurrent-writer cohort. Old + new code locking DIFFERENT lock domains do NOT mutually exclude — concurrent writers can race even though each ALONE acquires its lock correctly. Mitigations (pick ONE before any concurrent rollout): **(i) transitional dual-lock**: acquire both old AND new protocol locks in series during the transition window; **(ii) runtime version-marker**: write protocol-version byte to lockfile + fail-closed on read-version-mismatch (FORWARD-REF for v2 lock protocol bump); **(iii) single-version cohort barrier**: coordinate cohort upgrade via deployment system before any new-version runs. Currently sss has ZERO consumers of `_atomic_jsonl_append.py` (FORWARD-REF wire-up Ships 2-5 pending per `tmp/wave22-orchestrator-salvage-jsonl-mode-bits-audit-2026-05-05.md`), so the version-skew window is THEORETICAL until first hook migrates to use the module. Future protocol bumps MUST honor one of (i)/(ii)/(iii). HOLD the lock across BOTH (a) JSON-encoding the record AND (b) the single low-level `os.write(fd, encoded_bytes)` — never use buffered text writes (`f.write(json.dumps(rec) + "\n")` via Python file objects can split into multiple writes). **If JSON encoding raises (`UnicodeEncodeError`, non-serializable type), release the lock and propagate the exception — do not silently drop the record** (per Round-5 F-3 silent-record-drop finding). For records where **`len(encoded_bytes)` — the byte count AFTER JSON encoding, NOT the key count of the dict —** exceeds the platform atomic-write bound (≤PIPE_BUF 4KB POSIX; ≤512 bytes for Windows or treat the lock as the sole correctness boundary), reject the record OR shard via option (b) filename-namespace; do NOT silently truncate (per Round-5 F-5 measurement-target finding). After the write, `os.fsync(fd)` if durability is part of the contract (audit-trail JSONL needs fsync; ephemeral telemetry may skip); **if `os.fsync` raises (`EIO`, `ENOSPC`), propagate the exception — do not silently ignore a durability failure when durability is contracted** (per Round-5 F-4 fsync-failure finding). Use for SHARED JSONL log targets (`mcp_health.jsonl`, `subagent_transcripts.jsonl`, `codex_postcommit_reviews.jsonl` class) where consumers expect interleaved appends from multiple writers and unified-view semantics are required.

.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:144:- **Topic-named verdict file write-collision (operator-discipline anti-pattern)** — `.claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt` files write-collide if two parallel worktrees fire T1 on the same topic; second writer wins, silently overwriting the first. Mitigation: operator discipline (don't fire concurrent T1 on identical topic). SHA-prefixed `codex_review_HEAD_<sha8>.txt` files cannot collide (commit content differs ⇒ sha8 differs). Append-only JSONL is collision-tolerant per OS append semantics. Wave 18 architect audit (`tmp/wave18-architect-parallel-session-audit-2026-05-04.md` Surface 3+7+8) refuted the prior "each worktree should resolve `.claude/state/` relative to its own root" mitigation: under `tools/sss.ps1:CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` symbol-anchor `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude` env-pinning, all worktrees converge to shared parent state via 4 hardcoded `Path("Z:/claude-sota")` sites (`utils.py:50`, `_guard_base.py:63`, `shellcheck_guard.py:62`, `teammate_idle_gate.py:84`) PLUS Pattern B `parents[3]` resolution at `codex_t1_consult_gate.py:212`. Shared-state-by-design IS correct under CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR semantics — it enables single-grep MEMORY.md / verdict-trail / audit-JSONL aggregation. Operator discipline (named-topic uniqueness) replaces aspirational per-worktree state.

.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:4. **Classify a refuted gap-prescription as OVER and drop it.** Reserve HONEST-NON-FINDING for an agent/operator report that explicitly probed an assumed target and found no such target (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` OVER vs HNF distinction). .claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:- **Conflate OVER with HONEST-NON-FINDING** — these are distinct `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`: OVER = report claims more than git shows (refuted prescription class); HNF = report says "I probed for X, found nothing" (agent did the probe). A refuted gap-prescription where I probed and found the gap closed = OVER, NOT HNF (the agent didn't probe; I did).

.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:Ship-reports drift from git reality in BOTH directions OR fail to surface a finding entirely (HONEST-NON-FINDING — see §Reporting categories), and the failure-mode taxonomy keeps growing (agent flake, proxy restart, 429 retries, Windows loopback transients, model-side stalls, infrastructure deploys mid-cycle). The synthesis-layer recovery is the same single check across all of them: `git diff` + `git status` + smoke-import. Without this check, false-failure re-spawns waste 2-5 min each; false-success commits introduce silent no-ops into git history.
.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:## SubagentStop transcript-mining (4th OVER/UNDER/HNF detection axis — iter-109 closure)
.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:- **HONEST-NON-FINDING (transcript empty / parse failure)**: `parse_status != "ok"` — transcript missing or unparseable. Recovery: HNF disposition; do NOT re-spawn agent on the assumption transcript exists; treat axis as closed-without-finding.
.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:## Reporting categories — OVER, UNDER, HONEST-NON-FINDING
.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:| **HONEST-NON-FINDING** | Report says "I probed for X, found nothing" — when the brief assumed X exists | Pivot ship — DROP / REDIRECT / amend brief. The HNF IS the deliverable; do NOT re-spawn the agent to "find X anyway" |
.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:**Why HNF matters**: it's the highest-value report shape but easiest to mistake for "agent failed to ship." An agent that probes the brief's assumption and finds it wrong has saved a wrong-direction ship — that's a SUCCESS at the discipline layer, not a failure. The synthesis-layer recovery is to update the brief / drop the patch / redirect to the correct target — never to re-spawn under the same (now-refuted) assumption.
.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:**HNF as cite-vs-concept sibling**: HNF reports are often the empirical refutation of a brief-level cite-vs-concept failure. Brief asserts X exists at file:line; agent probes; X doesn't exist. Brief's assumption was a CONCEPT (loose familiarity) not a CITE (verified file:line). HNF surfaces this gap. (Cite-vs-concept semantic-equivalence remains FORWARD-REF in claude-sota; the parent CCC narrative is TIER-3 provenance only until ported with TIER-1/TIER-2 backing.)
.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:**Distinguishes from §Reporting categories above**: §Reporting categories classifies REPORT shape (OVER/UNDER/HNF — about agent's output). §Subclaim-type discriminator classifies SOURCE shape (UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY — about what's being verified). Orthogonal axes — a UPSTREAM-CLAIM probe may produce HNF, OVER, or UNDER report.
.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:- §Reporting categories (OVER/UNDER/HONEST-NON-FINDING) — covers verification-output classification AFTER spec is consumed; this section covers spec-input quality BEFORE consumption
.claude\rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:- TIER-3 port provenance: source rule with full parent cycle-N evidence trail (n=9 dogfood + cycle-484 subclaim-discriminator origin + cycle-562 deletion-commit-orphaned-caller HNF sibling): `Z:/claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` HEAD

1:# Operator PATH discoverability for `eee` launcher
5:> Microsoft Learn anchors retained inline; this file is bootstrap-class
9:The `bin/eee.cmd` shim ships with this runtime but is NOT auto-discoverable from a fresh PowerShell session until the operator adds `Z:\claude-sota-installed\bin` to PATH.
13:pwsh -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File Z:\claude-sota-installed\bin\install-path.ps1
16:This script (`bin/install-path.ps1`, Wave 50 Fire 40 ship) self-diagnoses 4 probes (shim presence / User-scope Path state / Process-scope Path state / install action), applies User-scope `SetEnvironmentVariable` if absent (no-op if present), and reports the exact next step depending on state. Cite-anchored to Microsoft Learn TIER-1 PowerShell 7.6 docs `https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_environment_variables?view=powershell-7.6` §"Create persistent environment variables in Windows" §"Set environment variables with SetEnvironmentVariable()" [VERIFIED 2026-05-06 via WebFetch].
18:**After running install-path.ps1, pick ONE path to launch**:
22:$env:Path = "Z:\claude-sota-installed\bin;$env:Path"
23:eee
28:# 1. Close ALL PowerShell + Windows Terminal windows
30:# 3. eee
33:**Why the shell-restart caveat exists**: Windows reads User-scope Path **at process start**, not on registry change. Existing PowerShell sessions inherit a snapshot from their parent process and don't refresh. This is a Windows-platform quirk, not a bug in the install. The `install-path.ps1` script's [3/4] probe surfaces this state explicitly so operators don't get confused when `eee` resolves in some shells but not others.
35:**Scope discipline** (per Microsoft Learn §"On Windows, environment variables can be defined in three scopes"):
36:- **User** scope (used by `install-path.ps1`): persists across PowerShell sessions for the operator only; no admin rights needed
37:- **Machine** scope: persists across sessions for ALL users; requires admin rights; reserved for system-wide installs (Tier 0 binaries like `claude.exe`, `codex.exe`); do NOT use for `eee` launcher
38:- **Process** scope: current PowerShell session only; Option A above
40:**Anti-pattern**: do NOT modify `Z:\claude-sota\bin` (sibling) PATH entry to point at installed runtime — that breaks sibling launcher (`sss`) discoverability. `install-path.ps1` appends `Z:\claude-sota-installed\bin` as a SEPARATE PATH entry. Both can coexist; `sss` and `eee` are distinct shim names.
42:**Pre-condition for `eee` to actually launch successfully** (after PATH is correct): hard-gate per `tools/eee.ps1:T0.1-T0.5` — claude.exe native install + 3 plugins enabled + 3 plugin caches present + python venv at `Z:/venvs/claude` + codex CLI on PATH. If any FAIL, `eee` exits 1 with error list. Use `EEE_BOOTSTRAP=1` env to bypass during initial install per cardinal-rule-7 graduated-unleash phase 1.

**Findings**
1. **Untracked credentials/runtime state are in the commit blast radius.** `git status --untracked-files=all` shows `.claude/.credentials.json`, `.claude/history.jsonl`, session env/scripts, file-history, metrics, paste-cache, `.config`, `.gateguard`, `.rustup`, `.semgrep`, and large `docs/outer research` trees. `.gitignore` only ignores `.codex/auth.json`, `.env*`, `auth/`, and a few cache dirs, not these Claude runtime files. This is a pre-commit blocker. See [.gitignore](Z:/claude-sota-installed/.gitignore:25).

2. **Manifest/provenance/settings do not agree on safety surfaces.** The request says `.claude/hooks/scripts/safety_guard.py` is new, but the file does not exist. `settings.json` still says the safety floor is preserved by `safety_guard.py`, while the manifest keeps `safety_guard.py` at `PLANNED-Path-A` and `agent_plan_readonly_bash_guard.py` at `PLANNED-Path-B-HNF-confirmed` despite the latter file existing. See [.claude/settings.json](Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/settings.json:23), [docs/sota-installed-manifest.md](Z:/claude-sota-installed/docs/sota-installed-manifest.md:193), and [docs/sota-installed-manifest.md](Z:/claude-sota-installed/docs/sota-installed-manifest.md:195).

3. **CR-7/settings state is internally contradictory.** `settings.json` has `permissions.defaultMode: "auto"`, but CLAUDE still defines Phase 1 as `default`, Phase 2 as `default` plus allow-list, and Phase 3 as `bypassPermissions`. The manifest bootstrap row also says Phase 1 is `default`. This means the runtime is already past the documented gate while `INSTALLED-AMBER`, `INSTALLED-PARTIAL`, and Tier-2 gaps remain. See [.claude/settings.json](Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/settings.json:22), [CLAUDE.md](Z:/claude-sota-installed/CLAUDE.md:66), and [docs/sota-installed-manifest.md](Z:/claude-sota-installed/docs/sota-installed-manifest.md:38).

4. **CWC manifest section is duplicated and malformed.** A new `### Section 17` was inserted near the top with installed rows, while the older `## Section 17` later still says the same primitives are `PLANNED`. The new table header has 5 columns but rows carry 6 columns, so markdown parsing/status extraction will be unreliable. See [docs/sota-installed-manifest.md](Z:/claude-sota-installed/docs/sota-installed-manifest.md:69) and [docs/sota-installed-manifest.md](Z:/claude-sota-installed/docs/sota-installed-manifest.md:471).

5. **Wave 62B close is not complete.** Prescription #2 says broader sibling-bleed audit passed, but many ported rule bodies still contain live `sss` / `sss-local` semantics and parent paths such as `Z:/claude/ccc/tools/status.py`. If those are provenance-only, they need explicit scoped whitelisting; otherwise they are sibling/parent bleed not covered by the 6-class pattern set.

**Missed Sibling-Bleed Classes**
Add scans for plain `sss\b`, `sss-local`, `tools/sss.ps1`, `bin/sss.cmd`, `Z:/claude/`, `Z:\claude\`, `ccc/tools/status.py`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, and case-insensitive/URL-encoded variants. Also distinguish provenance-header references from runtime instruction body references.

**Top Prescribed Edits**
1. **Narrow the commit set and harden `.gitignore`:** exclude `.claude/.credentials.json`, history/session/cache/state files, `.config`, `.gateguard`, `.rustup`, `.semgrep`, generated logs, and accidental research kit dumps unless intentionally tracked with manifest rows.

2. **Reconcile manifest/provenance/settings with actual files:** either add `safety_guard.py` with its HNF/effective-tier evidence or remove all claims that it landed; promote `agent_plan_readonly_bash_guard.py` rows from pending to installed with pinned SHA; merge the duplicate CWC Section 17 rows and fix the table column counts.

3. **Close cite-trail verification mechanically:** record actual compare output for cwc `e407c533...ffd563d6` drift on the five primitives, not just the conclusion. The local clone is shallow/current-only, so the “no content drift” claim is not independently verifiable from this tree.

**Highest-Blast-Radius Rollbacks**
1. **Permissions/MCP config:** revert `defaultMode: "auto"` to the documented phase, or update CLAUDE/manifest and prove all phase predicates. Remove unpinned `.mcp.json` Playwright/Serena additions until manifest rows and resolved pins exist.

2. **Runtime-state leakage:** do not commit untracked auth/session/history/cache files. Add ignore coverage first, then stage only intentional source/docs/install artifacts.

3. **Dormant hooks/rules/binaries:** if reverting Wave 62, remove `.claude/hooks/scripts/cwc/`, `.claude/agents/cwc/`, `.claude/agents/sota-researcher.md`, `.claude/rules/`, `.local/cwc/`, and the Wave 62 `.local/bin` binaries as one unit, then roll manifest/provenance rows back to planned/staged.
